{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.99306pt}{-10.19164pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\big)
is the inclusion into the first stage of the coequaliser. We thus obtain a map
\eta_{(X,\tau)}:E(X,\tau)\to X_{hC_{2}}
. The Bousfield–Kan homotopy orbits spectral sequence for
X
(which is no longer fringed as
X
is group-like), together with the assumption that
2
is inverted, gives a natural isomorphism
\pi_{*}(X_{hC_{2}})\cong H_{0}(C_{2};\pi_{*}(X))\cong\pi_{*}(X)_{C_{2}}
. Also by definition, we have that
\pi_{*}(E(X,\tau))\cong\mathrm{Im}(\tfrac{1+\tau}{2}:\pi_{*}(X)\to\pi_{*}(X))
. Then under these identifications, the map
\pi_{*}(\eta_{(X,\tau)})
is seen to be the natural isomorphism (away from
2
) that sends an element
\beta=\tfrac{1+\tau}{2}\alpha\in\pi_{*}(E(X,\tau))
to
[(1+\tau)\alpha]\in\pi_{*}(X)_{C_{2}}
. So
\eta_{(X,\tau)}
is the desired equivalence
X_{hC_{2}}\simeq_{[\frac{1}{2}]}E(X,\tau)
.

For the second part of the statement, we may assume without loss of generality that the homotopy
C_{2}
-equivariant equivalence
X\approx X^{\prime}
is induced by a single equivalence
f:X\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}X^{\prime}
(which is an
H
-map in the case when
X
and
X^{\prime}
are
H
-groups). Then, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1+\tau}{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1+\tau}{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1+\tau^{\prime}}{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1+\tau^{\prime}}{2}}

commutes up to homotopy, yielding the middle equivalence in

X_{hC_{2}}\simeq_{[\frac{1}{2}]}E(X,\tau)\simeq E(X^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})\simeq_{[\frac{1}{2}]}X^{\prime}_{hC_{2}}.

∎

Hence, when working with
2
inverted, we will often simply write
\simeq_{[\frac{1}{2}]}
instead of
\approx
. Even though we are not going to use it explicitly, it is good to have the following result as a rule of thumb, as it captures pretty well the effect that localising away from
2
has in the
C_{2}
-equivariant stable setting.

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
X
be a
C_{2}
-spectrum and let the prime
2
be inverted. Then the homotopy type of
\Omega^{\infty}(X_{hC_{2}})
(as a space) is completely determined by the homotopy type of the space
\Omega^{\infty}X
and the homotopy classes of the maps
t:\Omega^{\infty}X\to\Omega^{\infty}X
and
+:\Omega^{\infty}X\times\Omega^{\infty}X\to\Omega^{\infty}X
, where
t\in C_{2}
is the generator.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.3
, the homotopy type away from
2
of
(\Omega^{\infty}X)_{hC_{2}}\simeq_{[\frac{1}{2}]}E(\Omega^{\infty}X,t)
is of course determined by such data. The claim now follows because the natural map
(\Omega^{\infty}X)_{hC_{2}}\to\Omega^{\infty}(X_{hC_{2}})
is an equivalence away from
2
(this is a consequence of the Bousfield–Kan homotopy orbits spectral sequence).
∎

The following result, though unrelated to what has been discussed so far in this section, will be useful later on. Given an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-space
X
, we will write
X^{\mathrm{op}}
for
X
equipped with the opposite
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-structure. An
anti-involution

\tau
on an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-space
X
is an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-map
\tau:X\to X^{\mathrm{op}}
whose square equals the identity of
X
(noting that
(X^{\mathrm{op}})^{\mathrm{op}}\cong X
). Up to equivalence, there is a canonical way of delooping such an anti-involution.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
X
be an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-space. There is a natural equivalence

\iota:B(X^{\mathrm{op}})\simeq BX.

such that, for any anti-involution
\tau
on
X
, the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{B}\tau:BX}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B(X^{\mathrm{op}})\overset{\iota}{\simeq}BX}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\tau}

is an involution on
BX
.

Proof.

For each
k\geq 0
, the map
\mathbb{E}_{1}(k)\to\pi_{0}(\mathbb{E}_{1}(k))
is an equivalence, and hence there is a natural zig-zag of equivalences of
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B(\pi_{0}(\mathbb{E}_{1}),\mathbb{E}_{1},X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B(\mathbb{E}_{1},\mathbb{E}_{1},X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

But the
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-structure on the left hand side factors through the associative operad
\mathcal{A}ss:=\pi_{0}(\mathbb{E}_{1})
, so for simplicity, we may assume that
X
is strictly associative. The equivalence
\iota
is then induced on the realisation of the nerve
N_{\bullet}X
by the maps

X^{q}\times\Delta^{q}\longrightarrow X^{q}\times\Delta^{q},\quad(x_{1},\dots,x_{q},r)\longmapsto(x_{q},\dots,x_{1},\Phi_{q}(r)),

where
\Phi_{q}:\Delta^{q}\cong\Delta^{q}
is the linear homeomorphism induced by reversing the order of the vertices. It is easy to check that the map
\overline{B}\tau
indeed defines an involution on
BX
.∎

5.2.
From the
h
-cobordism spectrum to spaces of
h
-cobordisms

All throughout this section, assume that
d=\dim M\geq 5
; this condition will not be a problem later, as all of the results in this section will be used only once our original manifold
M
has been stabilised sufficiently many times.

We now recall Vogell’s model for spaces of
h
-cobordisms (cf.
[
Vog85
, p. 296]
). A
partition
of a manifold
M^{d}
is a triple
(W,F,V)
, where
W
is a codimension zero submanifold of
M\times[-1,1]
,
V
is the closure of the complement of
W
and
F^{d}:=W\cap V
. For technical reasons, we require
F
to be standard near
\partial M\times[-1,1]
, and that it intersects it in
\partial M\times\{0\}
. Let
H(M)_{\bullet}
denote the simplicial set a
p
-simplex of which is a (locally trivial smooth) family of partitions of
M
parametrised by
\Delta^{p}
such that
W
is an
h
-cobordism from
M\times\{-1\}\times\Delta^{p}
to
F
. Set
H(M):=|H(M)_{\bullet}|
and write
H^{s}(M)\subset H(M)
for the connected component containing the trivial partition
*=(M\times[-1,0],M\times\{0\},M\times[0,1])
. There is a canonical involution
\iota_{H}
given by turning upside down partitions. Namely

\iota_{H}:H(M)\longrightarrow H(M),\quad\rho=(W,F,V)\longrightarrow\rho^{*}:=(V^{*},F^{*},W^{*}),

where
W^{*}
,
F^{*}
and
V^{*}
are respectively the images of
W
,
F
and
V
under the reflection
r=\mathrm{Id}_{M}\times-1
. For the smooth case, we will also need a small variant of this
h
-cobordism space, denoted
H_{\mathsf{col}}(M)
, a point of which consists of a partition
\rho=(W,F,V)\in H(M)
together with a bicollar of
F
for
W
and
V
which is standard near
\partial M\times[-1,1]
. The forgetful map
H_{\mathsf{col}}(M)\to H(M)
is a weak equivalence by the contractibility of the space of collars. In this section we construct a homotopy
C_{2}
-equivariant
(\phi(d)+1)
-connected map

(5.2)

\mathrm{alex}:H(M)\longrightarrow\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{H}(M)

which generalises the map
B(\mathrm{alex})
of Proposition
3.8
. We first recall an important construction.

5.2.1.
The geometric Eilenberg swindle

An
h
-cobordism
W:M\hskip 2.0pt\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overset{h}{\leadsto}$}\hskip 2.0ptM^{\prime}
induces a unique (up to contractible choice) bounded diffeomorphism

(5.3)

ES_{W}:M\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}\cong M^{\prime}\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}

as follows: choose embeddings
i_{r}:W\xhookrightarrow{}M\times I
rel
M\times\{0\}
and
i_{\ell}:W\xhookrightarrow{}M^{\prime}\times I
rel
M^{\prime}\times\{1\}
(these are essentially partitions for
M
and
M^{\prime}
, which amount to no homotopical information by the contractibility of the space of collars). Write
V_{r}:=\overline{M\times I-i_{r}(W)}
and
V_{\ell}:=\overline{M^{\prime}\times I-i_{\ell}(W)}
; both of these manifolds are
h
-cobordisms
M^{\prime}\hskip 2.0pt\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overset{h}{\leadsto}$}\hskip 2.0ptM
, and in fact they are diffeomorphic relative to
both
ends since

V_{\ell}\cong V_{\ell}\cup_{M}M\times I\cong V_{\ell}\cup_{M}W\cup_{M^{\prime}}V_{r}\cong M^{\prime}\times I\cup_{M^{\prime}}V_{r}\cong V_{r}\quad\mathrm{rel}\ M^{\prime}\sqcup M.

Then the
Eilenberg swindle
diffeomorphism
ES_{W}
is given by the composition

ES_{W}:M\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}=\dots\cup_{M^{\prime}}V_{r}\cup_{M}W\cup_{M^{\prime}}V_{r}\cup_{M}\dots\cong\dots\cup_{M^{\prime}}V_{\ell}\cup_{M}W\cup_{M^{\prime}}V_{\ell}\cup_{M}\dots=M^{\prime}\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}.

Clearly, by construction,
ES_{W}
is bounded by
1
and unique up to contractible choice.

5.2.2.
The map (
5.2
)

Fix an embedding
M^{d}\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N}\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}
and recall that
B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}(M)
admits a model as the moduli space of manifolds embedded in
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}
which are abstractly diffeomorphic to
M
relative to the boundary
\partial M
. Similarly
B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}^{b}(M\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}})
is the moduli space of manifolds embedded in
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}
which are abstractly diffeomorphic to
M\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}

boundedly
with respect to the

\textstyle\mathbb{R}

-direction and relative to the boundary
\partial M\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}
(this is proved in Appendix
A.2
). For the remaining of this section, we will denote by
\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
the bounded direction, i.e., the last coordinate in
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}=:{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
. There is a natural map
-\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}:B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}(M)\xhookrightarrow{}B\mathrm{Diff}^{b}_{\partial}(M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}})
given by sending a manifold
N\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}
to
N\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
. In fact, in light of (
5.3
), this map extends to

-\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}:\coprod_{[M^{\prime}]}B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}(M^{\prime})\longrightarrow B\mathrm{Diff}^{b}_{\partial}(M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}),\quad N\longmapsto N\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}},

where the coproduct in the domain runs over all diffeomorphism classes of manifolds
M^{\prime}
with boundary
\partial M
that are
h
-cobordant to
M
rel
\partial M
. The map
\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
is the value of the morphism
0\to\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
in
\mathcal{J}_{0}
under

\widehat{B}:\mathcal{J}_{0}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Top}_{*},\quad\widehat{B}(V):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\coprod_{[M^{\prime}]}B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}(M^{\prime}),&V=0,\\[5.0pt]
B(V)=B\mathrm{Diff}^{b}_{\partial}(M\times V),&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Clearly
\widehat{B}
is an orthogonal functor and we will write
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}(M)
for its first derivative, which is canonically equivalent to
\mathbf{H}(M)
. The Alexander trick-like map (
5.2
) will factor through

\widehat{\mathbf{H}}(M)_{0}\xhookrightarrow{}\Omega^{\infty}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}(M)\simeq\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{H}(M)
.

Suppose we are given some partition
\rho=(W,F,V)\in H(M)
of
M\times\underline{[-1,1]}\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
. Then
W
is an
h
-cobordism from
M
to
F
rel boundary, and so the manifold
F\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N}\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}
gives rise to a point in
B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}(F)\subset\widehat{B}(0)
; more precisely, the image of the embedding

i_{\rho}:F\subset M\times I\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N+1}\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty},

is a point in
B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}(F)
, where the isomorphism
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N+1}
identifies
\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
with the last coordinate in
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N+1}
. We now construct a point in
B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}^{b}(M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}})
by
extending
W
towards infinity
. Consider the embedding of
F\times[0,1]
into
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N}\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
given by

R:F\times[0,1]\xhookrightarrow{}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N}\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}},\quad(x,t)\longmapsto\underline{e}+(\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N}}\times Q_{-\pi t/2})(x-\underline{e}),

where
Q_{\theta}:{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
is the rotation matrix
\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta&-\sin\theta\\
\sin\theta&\cos\theta\end{pmatrix}
, and
\underline{e}
denotes the unit length vector in
\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
. Write
r:=R\mid_{F\times\{1\}}:F\hookrightarrow{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N+1}\times\underline{\{1\}}
, and consider

S:F\times[1,+\infty)\xhookrightarrow{}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N+1}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}},\quad(x,t)\longmapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}r(x)+(1-t)\cdot e_{N+1}+(t-1)\cdot\underline{e},&t\in[1,2],\\
r(x)-e_{N+1}+(t-1)\cdot\underline{e},&t\geq 2.\end{array}\right.

Finally consider the region
D\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
given by tuples
(u,v)
with

u\geq 0,\quad u\leq 2-v,\quad\text{and if $0\leq u\leq 1$, then $u\leq(1-(v-1)^{2}))^{1/2}$.}

Then we define a topological manifold
\widehat{a}(\rho)\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N+1}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
, depicted in Figure
2
, by

\widehat{a}(\rho):=M\times\{0\}\times(-\infty\times-1]\cup W\cup R(F\times[0,1])\cup S(F\times[1,+\infty))\cup\partial M\times D.

Figure 2
.
Depiction of the topological manifold
\widehat{a}(\rho)
for
\rho\in H^{s}(M)
.

Now if
\rho
is a collared partition, i.e. a point in
H_{\mathsf{col}}^{s}(M)
, one can use the collar of
F
to smooth out the corners of the topological manifold
\widehat{a}(\rho)
, and thus obtain a smooth manifold
\widetilde{a}(\rho)\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N+1}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
with the same boundary as
M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
, and which is boundedly diffeomorphic to
M\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}
relative to the boundary. This construction can be done simplex-wise in
H_{\mathsf{col}}(M)_{\bullet}\simeq H(M)_{\bullet}
, and so up to weak equivalence gives rise to an Alexander trick-like map

(5.4)

\displaystyle\mathrm{alex}:H(M)

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}(M)_{0}:=\operatorname{hofib}\left(\coprod_{[M^{\prime}]}B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}(M^{\prime})\to B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}^{b}(M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}})\right),

\displaystyle\rho=(W,F,V)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\left(i_{\rho}(F),\ \gamma_{W}:[-\infty,\infty]\ni t\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}},&t=-\infty,\\
\widetilde{a}(\rho)-t\cdot\underline{e},&-\infty<t<+\infty,\\
i_{\rho}(F)\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}},&t=+\infty.\end{array}\right.\right),

where we can regard the path
\gamma_{W}
as a
1
-simplex in
B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}^{b}(M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}})_{\bullet}
from
M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
to
F\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
. Then (
5.2
) is

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{alex}:H(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbf{H}}(M)_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}(M)\simeq\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{H}(M)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{alexhcob})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Proposition 5.6
.

The map (
5.4
) is indeed an equivalence. Therefore, (
5.2
) is
(\phi(d)+1)
-connected.

Proof.

Noting the equivalence
H^{s}(M)\simeq BC(M)
(cf.
[
Vog85
, Prop. 2.1]
), the map (
5.4
) is, up to homotopy, a (non-connected) delooping of the Alexander trick-like equivalence
C(M)\simeq\Omega(\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}^{b}(M\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}})/\mathrm{Diff}(M))
of
[
WW88
, Prop. 1.10]
, and therefore it is an equivalence on basepoint components.

Given a diffeomorphism class
[M^{\prime}]
of manifolds
h
-cobordant to
M
(rel boundary), denote by
H(M,M^{\prime})
the collection of path components in
H(M)
consisting of (collared) partitions
\rho=(W,F,V)
with
F\in[M^{\prime}]
. A choice of basepoint
\rho_{0}=(W_{0},F_{0},V_{0})\in H(M,M^{\prime})
, a bicollar
c_{0}:F_{0}\times[-\epsilon,\epsilon]\xhookrightarrow{}M\times[-1,1]
and a diffeomorphism
\phi_{0}:M^{\prime}\cong F_{0}
gives rise to an equivalence
H(M^{\prime},M^{\prime})\overset{\sim}{\to}H(M,M^{\prime})
which sends a partition
\rho^{\prime}=(W^{\prime},F^{\prime},V^{\prime})
of
M^{\prime}\times[-1,1]
to the partition of
M\times[-1,1]
whose
F
-part is the image of
F^{\prime}
under

M^{\prime}\times[-1,1]\xrightarrow{\phi_{0}\times\cdot\epsilon}F^{\prime}\times[-\epsilon,\epsilon]\xhookrightarrow{c_{0}}M\times[-1,1].

By the
s
-cobordism theorem, a homotopy inverse
H(M,M^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}H(M^{\prime},M^{\prime})
is given by the same kind of map for a choice of basepoint
\rho^{\prime}_{0}=(W^{\prime}_{0},F^{\prime}_{0},V^{\prime}_{0})\in H(M^{\prime},M^{\prime})
such that
\phi_{0}(W^{\prime}_{0})
is an
h
-cobordism starting at
F_{0}
with the same torsion as
V_{0}
(the inverse of
W_{0}
).

Observe also that the choices
(\rho_{0},c_{0})
and
\phi_{0}
above give a preferred path in
B\mathrm{Diff}^{b}_{\partial}(M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}})=B\mathrm{Diff}^{b}_{\partial}(M^{\prime}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}})
from
M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
to
M^{\prime}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
; namely, it is the composition of
\gamma_{W_{0}}
, as defined in (
5.4
), with the mapping cylinder of
\phi_{0}\times\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}}
. These preferred paths give rise to the ”change of basepoint” equivalences in the right column of the homotopy commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{hofib}_{M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}}\left(\coprod_{[M^{\prime}]}B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}(M^{\prime})\to B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}^{b}(M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}})\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{[M^{\prime}]}H(M,M^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{[M^{\prime}]}\operatorname{hofib}_{M^{\prime}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}}\left(B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}(M^{\prime})\to B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}^{b}(M^{\prime}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}})\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{[M^{\prime}]}H(M^{\prime},M^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{[M^{\prime}]}\mathbf{H}(M^{\prime})_{0},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{alexhcob})}

\simeq

\simeq

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{[M^{\prime}]}\mathrm{alex}}

where
\mathrm{alex}:H(M^{\prime},M^{\prime})\to\mathbf{H}(M^{\prime})_{0}\subset\widehat{\mathbf{H}}(M^{\prime})_{0}
is the restriction to
H(M^{\prime},M^{\prime})\subset H(M^{\prime})
of the map (
5.4
) for
M=M^{\prime}
. Moreover if
d\geq 5
, this map is an isomorphism in
\pi_{0}
(in fact it is an equivalence by the argument above); indeed, the inverse

\pi_{0}\left(\mathrm{Diff}^{b}_{\partial}(M^{\prime}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}})/\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}(M^{\prime})\right)\longrightarrow\pi_{0}(H(M^{\prime},M^{\prime}))\subset\mathrm{Wh}(M)

sends the coset
[\phi]
of some bounded diffeomorphism
\phi\in\mathrm{Diff}^{b}_{\partial}(M^{\prime}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}})
(say bounded by
1/2
for simplicity) to a partition of
M^{\prime}\times[-1,1]
whose
W
-part is the
h
-cobordism obtained as the region in
M^{\prime}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
between
M^{\prime}\times\{0\}
and
\phi(M^{\prime}\times\{1\})
(cf.
[
WW88
, Cor. 5.4]
). It follows that the lower horizontal map, and hence (
5.4
), is an isomorphism in
\pi_{0}
. This proves the first claim.

The second claim is a consequence of the fact that
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}(M)_{0}\xhookrightarrow{}\Omega^{\infty}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}(M)
is
(\phi(d)+1)
-connected: indeed this map is
\phi(d)
-connected upon looping once by
[
WW88
, Lem. 1.12]
, and is an isomorphism in
\pi_{0}
by the analysis above and
[
WW88
, Prop. 1.8 & Cor. 5.3]
.
∎

Proposition 5.7
.

The map (
5.4
) is
C_{2}
-equivariant up to homotopy. Therefore, so is (
5.2
).

Proof.

We will give an argument only in the topological setting; in the smooth setting one works with
H_{\mathsf{col}}(M)
instead to smooth out corners, and uses smooth approximations of the continuous functions that will appear in proof below. We will however state the argument in the smooth setting to simplify notation. We will also assume at any point in the argument where it is necessary that a partition
(W,F,V)
is equipped with some bicollar of
F
in
W
and
V
. We adopt the convention that
\pm\infty+r\equiv\pm\infty
for any real number
r\in{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}
.

The Weiss–Williams involution on
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}(M)_{0}
is induced by the identity on
\coprod_{[M^{\prime}]}B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}(M^{\prime})
and the involution
U\mapsto U^{*}=(\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}}\times(-1)_{\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}})(U)
on
B\mathrm{Diff}^{b}_{\partial}(M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}})
. Then for
\rho=(W,F,V)\in H^{s}(M)
,

\tau_{WW}\circ\mathrm{alex}(W,F,V)=(i_{\rho}(F),\gamma_{W}^{*}),\qquad\mathrm{alex}\circ\iota_{H}(W,F,V)=(i_{\rho^{*}}(F^{*}),\gamma_{V^{*}}),

where
\gamma_{W}^{*}(t):=(\gamma_{W}(t))^{*}
. We have depicted the paths
\gamma_{W}^{*}
and
\gamma_{V^{*}}
in Figure
3
. We need to find a path
\eta:[-1,1]\to B\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}(M)
from
i_{\rho}^{*}(F^{*})
to
i_{\rho}(F)
and a homotopy
\{H_{s}(-)\}_{-1\leq s\leq 1}
from
\gamma_{V^{*}}(-)
to
\gamma_{W}^{*}(-)
such that
H_{s}(-\infty)=M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
and
H_{s}(+\infty)=\eta(s)\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
for all
s\in[-1,1]
.

Figure 3
.
Paths
\gamma_{W}^{*}
and
\gamma_{V^{*}}
in
B\mathrm{Diff}^{b}_{\partial}(M\times\underline{\mathbb{R}})
. The arrow indicates the direction of the path as time increases.

For
\eta
, we use the last two coordinates in
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N+2}
to do a half rotation of that plane. More explictly,
\eta(s):=(\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N}}\times Q_{\pi\cdot(s+1)/2})(i_{\rho}(F))
where
Q_{\theta}:{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{2}\cong{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{2}
is as before. View
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}
as
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}\times\underline{\{0\}}\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
and write
N:=\bigcup_{s\in[-1,1]}\eta(s)+s\cdot\underline{e}
. For
X\subset{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
, write
X\mid_{[a,b]}
for
X\cap({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{\infty}\times\underline{[a,b]})
. Then consider the compact manifold

U_{\rho}:=\left(\widehat{a}(\rho^{*})\mid_{[-1,2]}-3\cdot\underline{e}\right)\cup N\cup\left((\widehat{a}(\rho)\mid_{[-1,2]})^{*}+3\cdot\underline{e}\right)

depicted in Figure
4
. We can straighten
U_{\rho}-(V^{*}\cup W^{*})\cong F^{*}\times(-3,3)
to a surface of revolution in
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N}\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{2}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
about a rotation in
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{3}\cong{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{2}\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
. Using the bicollar of
F^{*}
in
W^{*}
and
V^{*}
to gradually eat up this surface of revolution, we obtain a canonical path
\psi
from
U_{\rho}
to
M\times\underline{[-4,4]}=M\times\underline{[-4,-1]}\cup V^{*}\cup W^{*}\cup M\times\underline{[1,4]}
in the moduli space of manifolds inside
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N+2}\times\underline{[-4,4]}
which are diffeomorphic to
M\times\underline{[-4,4]}
relative to its boundary
M\times\underline{\{-4\}}\cup\partial M\times\underline{[-4,4]}\cup M\times\underline{\{4\}}
.

Figure 4
.
Depiction of the manifold
U_{\rho}
. Proceed with caution: the part of the picture corresponding to
N
takes place in an extra dimension that we are unable to depict accurately.

We now describe the homotopy
\{H_{s}(-)\}_{-1\leq s\leq 1}
. Fix some homeomorphism
l:[-1,1]\cong[-\infty,+\infty]
, and assume that the path
\psi
from
M\times\underline{[-4,4]}
to
U_{\rho}
just described is parametrised by
[-\infty,+\infty]
. Then
H_{s}(-)
is the concatenation of two paths
H_{s}^{(1)}(-)
and
H_{s}^{(2)}(-)
in
B\mathrm{Diff}^{b}_{\partial}(M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}})
: roughly speaking the path
H_{s}^{(1)}(-)
performs
\psi(-)
on
M\times\underline{[-4,4]}+l(s)\cdot\underline{e}\subset M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
(if
s=\pm 1
,
H^{(1)}_{s}(-)
is constant on
M\times\underline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}}
). The path
H_{s}^{(2)}(-)
starts at
H^{(1)}_{s}(+\infty)
, and sends
H^{(1)}_{s}(+\infty)\mid_{(-\infty,l(s)+s]}
and
H^{(1)}_{s}(+\infty)\mid_{[l(s)+s,+\infty)}
towards
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{N+2}\times\underline{\pm\infty}
, respectively, extending by
H^{(1)}_{s}(+\infty)\mid_{l(s)+s}
times an interval of diverging length. The resulting paths
H_{\pm 1}(-)=H^{(1)}_{\pm 1}(-)\cdot H^{(2)}_{\pm 1}(-)
are reparametrisations of
\gamma_{V^{*}}
and
\gamma_{W^{*}}
(the reparametrisations only depend on our choice of homeomorphism
l:[-1,1]\cong[-\infty,+\infty]
and the parametrisation of the path
\psi
). Thus
\eta
and
H
give rise to the required homotopy
\tau_{WW}\circ\mathrm{alex}\simeq\mathrm{alex}\circ\iota_{H}
.
∎

Corollary 5.8
.

The Weiss-Williams involution on
\pi_{0}^{s}(\mathbf{H}(M))\cong\pi_{0}(H(M))\cong\mathrm{Wh}(\pi_{1}M)
corresponds to the rule
\kappa\mapsto(-1)^{d-1}\overline{\kappa}
, where
\overline{(-)}
is Milnor’s involution
[
Mil66
]
on
\mathrm{Wh}(\pi_{1}(M))
(see Warning
5.18
).

Proof.

The isomorphism
\pi_{0}(H(M))\cong\mathrm{Wh}(\pi_{1}M)
sends the class
[\rho]
of a partition
\rho=(W,F,V)
to the Whitehead torsion
\tau(W,M)
of
W
with respect to
M
. The claim follows from Proposition
5.7
, the
duality formula
of
[
Mil66
, §10]
and the fact that
\tau(V,F)
is roughly
-\tau(W,M)
(cf.
[
Mil66
, Lem. 7.8]
).
∎

Recall that
H^{s}(M)\simeq BC(M)
. Now if
P\subset M
is a codimension zero embedding and
p\leq d-3
(in the notation of Theorem
A
), then

by the isotopy extension sequence (

), and therefore

inherits an involution
\iota_{H}
up to weak equivalence. The map (

) is functorial with respect to codimension zero embeddings, so the following diagram is commutative:

(5.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{s}(\overline{M-P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{H}(\overline{M-P})_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}(\overline{M-P}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{s}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{H}(M)_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}(M)).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{alex}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{alex}}

Corollary 5.9
.

If
\dim P=d
, the vertical homotopy fibre of the horizontal compositions in (

) gives a map

The connectivity of this map is the content of Proposition

. It is homotopy

and

.
∎

Warning 5.10
.

There is a canonical involution
\iota_{C}
in the concordance space
C(M)
given by turning upside down a concordance and precomposing by the inverse of the top diffeomorphism (see e.g.
[
Vog85
, p. 296]
). The restriction map
C(M)\to C\mathrm{Emb}(P,M)
is
not

C_{2}
-equivariant with respect to
\iota_{C}
and
\iota_{H}
—rather, it is anti-equivariant. This may seem to contradict
[
Vog85
, Prop. 2.2]
, but what Vogell really proves there is that there is a homotopy
C_{2}
-equivariant equivalence
C(M)\approx\Omega^{\sigma}H(M):=\mathrm{Map}_{*}(S^{\sigma},H(M))
, where we recall
S^{\sigma}
stands for the representation sphere of the
1
-dimensional sign representation
\sigma
. This is due to an extra flip in the loop component that he introduces at the end of the proof of the proposition.

From

-theory

.

Remark 5.12
.

Though it probably is, we do not claim the equivalence above is one of infinite loop spaces.

Lemma 5.13
.

For each
k\geq 0
, there is a natural
C_{2}
-equivariant equivalence of spectra

(5.11)

e_{k}:\mathbf{H}(M\times I^{k})\simeq S^{k\cdot(\sigma-1)}\wedge\mathbf{H}(M).

such that the following square is homotopy commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{H}(M\times I^{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\sigma-1}\wedge\mathbf{H}(M\times I^{k-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{k\cdot(\sigma-1)}\wedge\mathbf{H}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{k\cdot(\sigma-1)}\wedge\mathbf{H}(M).}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{1}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{S}^{\sigma-1}\wedge e_{k-1}}

Proof.

Set
e_{0}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbf{H}(M)}
. We may assume
k=1
, so that the above square is homotopy commutative by construction. Recall
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{a,b}:={\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{a}\oplus b\cdot\sigma
, and for any orthogonal functor
F(-)
let
C_{2}=O(1)
act on
F({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{a,b})
by the induced action. Finally let
B(-):=B\mathrm{Diff}^{b}_{\partial}(M\times(-))
and

. The Alexander trick-like map of
[

, Prop. 1.5]
is a

-equivariant map

\mathrm{alex}:\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}^{b}(M\times I\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{a,b})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Omega\mathrm{Diff}_{\partial}^{b}(M\times{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{a+1,b})

which, upon delooping, gives rise to a
C_{2}
-equivariant equivalence on basepoint components
B(\mathrm{alex.}):BI({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{a,b})\simeq_{0}\Omega B({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{a+1,b})
. Writing
\Xi
for the
C_{2}
-spectrum whose

-th space is

and with stabilisation maps

, we obtain a
C_{2}
-equivariant equivalence of spectra

(5.12)

B(\mathrm{alex}):\mathbf{H}(M\times I):=\Theta(BI)^{(1)}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Xi:=\{B^{(1)}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{1,n+1})\}_{n\geq 0}.

But now the stabilisation map
s_{1,0}:S^{\sigma}\wedge B^{(1)}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{0,n})\to B^{(1)}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.97}{$\displaystyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\textstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}{\scalebox{0.97}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{R}$}}}^{1,n})=\Xi_{n-1}
induces another

-equivariant equivalence of spectra

Composing these two equivalences gives the one in the statement.
∎

Vogell introduced in
[

, p. 298]
the

and

\Sigma_{\ell},\Sigma_{u}:H(M)\to H(M\times I)
. Roughly, the former sends a partition

to

, where

into a

\Sigma_{u}
does the same to

instead of

(see Figure

). We will only be interested in the lower stabilisation

, which we will denote by

for simplicity. Here’s how it interacts with the
h
-cobordism involution
\iota_{H}
.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let

stand for the ”stacking in the

-algebra structure

7

7

The technical assumption we imposed on a partition

in

. Then:

(a)\hskip 3.0pt\iota_{H}\Sigma+_{I}\Sigma\iota_{H}\simeq*:H(M)\to H(M\times I),\qquad(b)\hskip 3.0pt\iota_{H}\Sigma^{2}\simeq\Sigma^{2}\iota_{H}:H(M)\to H(M\times I^{2}).

∎

Proof of Theorem
5.11
.

We may assume without loss of generality that
\dim M\geq 5
, for if not replace it by
M\times I^{2k}
for
k\geq 3
. The effect this has on both sides in the equivalence of the statement is rather mild: as there is a homotopy

-equivariant equivalence

, it follows by Lemma

and Proposition

that there are equivalences of spectra

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}(M\times J^{2k})_{hC_{2}}

\displaystyle\simeq_{[\frac{1}{2}]}\mathbf{H}(M)_{hC_{2}},

\displaystyle\big(S^{(d+2k)\cdot(\sigma-1)-1}\wedge\mathbf{Wh}^{\mathrm{Diff}}(M\times J^{2k};\epsilon)\big)_{hC_{2}}

\displaystyle\simeq_{[\frac{1}{2}]}\big(S^{d\cdot(\sigma-1)-1}\wedge\mathbf{Wh}^{\mathrm{Diff}}(M;\epsilon)\big)_{hC_{2}}.

In the second equivalence we also use that
\mathbf{Wh}^{\mathrm{Diff}}(-)
and
\tau_{\epsilon}
are homotopy invariants of
(-)
.

Let
k\geq 0
and let
\xi
denote the
(d+k)
-spherical fibration corresponding to the stable tangent bundle
T(M\times I^{k})\oplus\epsilon^{1}
. If

(and hence

) is stably parallelisable, the involution
(-1)^{d+k}\tau_{\epsilon}
is homotopic to

by (

), and by
[

, Prop. Cor. 2.10]
it agrees with
\mathscr{T}
(and hence extends
\iota_{H}
). All in all, we obtain a zig-zag of homotopy
C_{2}
-equivariant maps

(5.13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{H}(M\times I^{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(M\times I^{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}\big(S^{(d+k)\cdot(\sigma-1)-1}\wedge\mathbf{Wh}^{\mathrm{Diff}}(M\times I^{k})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}(S^{k\cdot(\sigma-1)}\wedge\mathbf{H}(M))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}\big(S^{(d+k)\cdot(\sigma-1)-1}\wedge\mathbf{Wh}^{\mathrm{Diff}}(M)\big)}

\simeq

(
5.11
)

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{totalalexhcobmap})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{alex}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\dagger)}

\simeq

Every space involved in (
5.13
) is an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-group if
k\geq 1
: both

and
\Omega^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}(M\times I^{k}))
by stacking in the first of the
